Then run a time-varying gateway (field backbones per slice, aggregated from art_yrradSC27HtQ's topic-pair counts) with field fixed effects, declared non-identifiable in advance if within-field variation is too small. (C) Placebos: 200 connectivity- and degree-preserving rewired backbones, 1,000 permutations of gateway values across fields, and a head-to-head of degree, strength, betweenness, PageRank, closeness, k-core and phi_min eigenvector centrality (Holm-corrected). (D) Re-screen the O1 gains of the G variants with label coverage and a training-fold O1 base rate added to B5. (E) Power: minimum detectable delta-AUC at 80% power for 1,000, 2,000 and 4,000 episodes under a concept-cluster design effect, plus held-out concepts needed per group, computed both analytically and by simulation. (F) Corrected record tables for the paper. Everything goes to eval_out.json, one CI convention per table, with a pre-registered verdict ladder (REPLICATES / ATTENUATES / FIELD-TRAIT / FAILS).\",", "   \"runpod_compute_profile\": \"cpu_plus\",", "   \"builds_on\": \"This is a DEEPEN move. It builds entirely on iteration-1 outputs and needs no new data collection: 0 OpenAlex credits and 0 OpenRouter dollars. The executor reads these files in place (read-only). art_33_KKk_G8Gw5 = /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_Id7TLZ6r1C7M/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_4/: field_outcomes.csv (80 rows, 28 concepts; columns concept, group{CS,BGM,Med,Eng}, field (OpenAlex field name), n_W3, n_A, n_B, share_W3, n_outcome, share_outcome, R, log_n_W3, growth_j, gateway_j, phi_home_j, density_j, log_field_size); field_backbone.json (26 fields, field_ids 11-36, n_field, cooc, pmi, phi = max(PMI,0) with 78 positive edges, phi_min, gateway_eig normalised to max 1, gateway_deg, gateway_btw, gateway_eig_phimin); screen_result.json (field_level block: all_four_available, gateway_j, phi_home_j, density_j, size_controlled_gateway_j, size_controlled_all_three, log_field_size_alone_added; secondary_screens block with G_all, G_deg, G_btw, G_phimin, G_A, REL_home, RS, DOM_*, GATEWAY_REACH; per-group rho_B5); outcomes.csv (authoritative O1, O2r_m30/m50, O2r_resid, label_coverage_early, trunc); features.csv (B5 columns and G variants); next_field_entry.csv (not used here). Its code is reused verbatim, not rewritten: screen.py (logo_predict, _prep with training-fold median imputation, _auc, field_level with a concept-clustered FIXED-prediction bootstrap), backbone.py (PMI to phi to eigenvector centrality via networkx.eigenvector_centrality_numpy), and method.py lines ~95-115 (how gateway_j, phi_home_j and density_j are attached to a row, and the R rule R = 1 iff share_outcome >= 0.5*share_W3 and n_outcome >= 9). art_xp8BGBJZsxeI = .../iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_1/: results/field_outcomes.csv (367 rows, 46 concepts; concept, field in S2 names, j, R_j, n_j_early, n_j_late, log_n_j_early, growth_j, share_j, dev_group; R_j rate 0.763, base AUC 0.853); results/field_features.csv (rho_star, bg_LOR_j, n_child_j, has_data); results/features.csv and outcomes.csv (B5 inputs, A_h, home_s2, label_coverage_early); results/screen_result.json (rho_B5 = 0.834 and its per-group values, field_level delta +0.002); lineage.py (S2_FIELDS list of 23 s2-fos categories, S2_DEV map). art_yrradSC27HtQ = .../iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_3/: results/field_outcomes.csv (129 rows; field is a NUMERIC OpenAlex field id, group codes CS/MED/ENG/BIO, R_j, logn_j_early, growth_j, share_j, n_j_WO, share_j_WO, Dj, Fj, D_ratio, F_res); results/field_names.csv (id to name); results/outcomes.csv and features.csv (B5, rho_B5 = 0.770); results/screen_result.json (34-row 'portability' block, per-group delta-rho); results/topic_meta.csv (topic to field); scan/ckpt.npz plus scan/topic_ids.json (topic-pair co-occurrence counts per slice 2000-04/2005-09/2010-14 stored as flattened index arrays pk{s} and counts pc{s}, per-year topic totals bg) and backbone/slice{0,1,2}.npz (topic PMI edges a, b, w and W per slice). These last files are the zero-cost source of a TIME-VARYING field backbone. Negative findings that are built past and not retested: A*_h (null, delta-rho -0.006) and D_ratio (+0.006) are closed and appear only in the record tables. The iteration-1 fixed-prediction CIs (e.g. +0.103 [0.034, 0.167]) are superseded by the refit CIs computed here. If any file is missing, the executor records it in eval_out.json['missing_inputs'] and runs the blocks that remain. Blocks A-exp4, C, D and F need only gen_art_experiment_4; B-time-varying is the only block that needs gen_art_experiment_3/scan.\",", "-  \"metrics_descriptions\": \"UNIT AND OUTCOME. One row is an adoption episode (concept c, off-home field j). R_cj is binary retention as each experiment defined it (identical rule in all three: late share >= 0.5 x early share AND >= 9 late papers). The resampling unit is the CONCEPT everywhere. GROUPS: home groups CS, Eng, BGM, Med, harmonised across files (exp1 dev_group long names; exp3 CS/ENG/BIO/MED; exp4 CS/Eng/BGM/Med).\\n\\nSTEP 0: INPUT HARMONISATION (about 20 min). (0a) Attach backbone quantities to every row. exp4 already has them; recompute them from field_backbone.json and assert max abs diff < 1e-9 against its columns. exp3 maps numeric id to name via field_names.csv. exp1 uses a PRE-SPECIFIED S2-to-OpenAlex crosswalk, written into eval_out.json before any model is fitted: Computer Science->Computer Science; Engineering->Engineering; Medicine->Medicine; Chemistry->Chemistry; Materials Science->Materials Science; Physics->Physics and Astronomy; Mathematics->Mathematics; Environmental Science->Environmental Science; Psychology->Psychology; Agricultural and Food Sciences->Agricultural and Biological Sciences; Geology->Earth and Planetary Sciences; Economics->Economics, Econometrics and Finance; Business->Business, Management and Accounting; Biology->{Biochemistry, Genetics and Molecular Biology; Agricultural and Biological Sciences; Immunology and Microbiology; Neuroscience}; Sociology, Political Science, Education, Law, Linguistics, Geography->Social Sciences (Geography also Earth and Planetary Sciences); Philosophy, History, Art->Arts and Humanities. For a one-to-many mapping, gateway_j is the n_field-weighted mean of the mapped fields' gateway_eig (n_field from field_backbone.json). A sensitivity drops every row whose field is one-to-many or many-to-one (Biology, Geography and the 5 Social-Sciences S2 fields). (0b) phi_home_j is the mean of phi[h, j] over the concept's home field(s); density_j is recomputed with exp4's own function from the concept's early-window field presence; log_field_size = log(n_field[j]). (0c) B5 per concept is merged from each experiment's own features.csv (log early volume, growth, off-home share, entropy, reach; exp4 names log_count_W5, growth, offhome_share_W3, entropy_W3, reach_W3; map the other files' equivalents and list the mapping). (0d) Overlap report: number of concepts shared between each pair and all three; number of (concept, field) episodes shared; and, on shared episodes, agreement of R (Cohen's kappa and the 2x2 table). (0e) Union panel: de-duplicate by (concept, OpenAlex field). Priority is exp4 > exp3 > exp1 (venue labels before the s2-fos text classifier); keep a source flag. A sensitivity keeps only episodes whose R agrees across every file that contains them.\\n\\nBLOCK A: REPLICATION (primary metric = delta-AUC). Model: standardised L2 logistic regression (C = 1.0), leave-one-home-group-out (LOGO) out-of-fold predictions, training-fold median imputation. Both come from exp4's screen.py and are imported, not rewritten. delta-AUC = pooled OOF AUC(base + gateway_j) - pooled OOF AUC(base). Three nested baselines per dataset: M0 = own B_field (exp4 [log_n_W3, growth_j, share_W3]; exp1 [log_n_j_early, growth_j, share_j]; exp3 [logn_j_early, growth_j, share_j]); M1 = M0 + B5; M2 = M1 + log_field_size + phi_home_j + density_j (+ bg_LOR_j in exp1, where it exists). CI (the ONLY reported CI convention for new results): concept-clustered REFIT bootstrap with 2,000 draws. Resample concepts with replacement, give each duplicate a fresh cluster id, rerun the full LOGO fit of both models, recompute pooled OOF delta-AUC, and take percentile 90% and 95% intervals and P(delta <= 0). If a resample leaves a test group with one class, drop that group's rows from that draw's pooled AUC and count such draws (report the count; if it exceeds 5%, switch to stratified-by-group concept resampling and say so). Also report per group: AUC_base, AUC_cand, delta, and the sign count out of 4. As a check, reproduce exp4's reported numbers exactly: fixed-prediction delta = 0.10254 and size-controlled delta = 0.10222. Datasets: exp4 (80), exp1 (367, and the crosswalk-clean subset), exp3 (129), union panel (all de-duplicated episodes), and a NEW-EPISODES-ONLY panel (union rows absent from exp4's 80), which is the cleanest replication. Pooling across datasets: DerSimonian-Laird random-effects estimate of delta-AUC over the three dataset estimates, with I^2 and tau^2. It is labelled DESCRIPTIVE because the concepts overlap; the union-panel refit CI is the inferential number. Secondary effect-size metrics: standardised logistic coefficient of gateway_j in M2 with its cluster-bootstrap CI; Brier score change; and the Spearman correlation of gateway_j with log_field_size, phi_home_j and density_j across rows.\\n\\nBLOCK B: TRAIT CONFOUND. (B1) Field retention propensity P_cj. For a TEST row, P_cj is the shrunken mean of R over TRAINING-fold rows in field j (the test group is excluded, hence also concept c): P = (sum R + a*pbar)/(n + a), with a = 2 as primary and a in {0, 5} as sensitivity, and pbar = training-fold mean R. For TRAINING rows the same quantity excludes the row's own concept. Two versions: within-dataset, and cross-dataset pooled (rows of all three files, always excluding concept c everywhere and the test group; s2-fos rows enter through the crosswalk). Metrics: delta-AUC of gateway_j over M2 + P (refit CI); the correlation of gateway_j with P across fields; and delta-AUC of P alone over M2, which shows how much the trait carries by itself. (B2) Field-intercept decomposition, the static FE test. Because gateway_j is one number per field, it is perfectly collinear with field dummies, so a static FE test is unidentifiable by construction, and this is stated in the output. Instead: stage 1 fits an L2 logistic model R ~ M1 + field dummies (C = 1) on each dataset and the union panel and extracts field effects u_j (fields with >= 3 rows). Stage 2 is a WLS regression of u_j on gateway_j (weights = n_rows_j), reporting slope, R^2 and a 2,000-permutation p-value that shuffles gateway values across fields; the same is repeated with log_field_size in stage 2. Also report the field ICC from a random-intercept model (statsmodels BinomialBayesMixedGLM with crossed field and concept variance components, or the latent-scale ICC = tau^2/(tau^2 + pi^2/3) from a field-only random-intercept fit) and the share of tau^2_field that gateway_j removes. (B3) Time-varying gateway with field FE. Build 26-field backbones for slices 2000-04 and 2005-09 by aggregating exp3's topic-pair counts (pk/pc in scan/ckpt.npz, topic to field via topic_meta.csv and topic_ids.json) to field-pair counts C_fg, field marginals n_f (sum of per-topic totals over the slice years, or row sums) and the slice total W. Then PMI = log(C_fg W/(n_f n_g)), phi = max(PMI, 0) and eigenvector centrality, exactly as in exp4's backbone.py. VALIDATION GATE: Spearman(slice-2000-04 gateway, exp4 1998-2002 gateway_eig) across 26 fields must be >= 0.7, otherwise the aggregation is reported as non-comparable and B3 uses only the exp3-built series for both slices. Assign each episode the slice by onset (t0 2003-2005 -> 2000-04; t0 2006-2009 -> 2005-09). The outcome window t0+6.. begins in 2009 or later, so no slice overlaps it. IDENTIFIABILITY GATE, pre-registered: compute SD_within (the SD of gateway_{j,slice} minus the field mean, over rows) and SD_between. If SD_within/SD_between < 0.10, or fewer than 8 fields have rows in both slices, B3 is reported as NOT IDENTIFIABLE on iteration-1 data. The test then passes to the iteration-2 common-panel experiment, which the hypothesis already commissions (3 slices, >= 4,000 episodes). Otherwise fit logistic R ~ M1 + field dummies + gateway_{j,slice}, report the coefficient with a concept-clustered refit bootstrap CI (2,000) and the permutation p (shuffle the slice labels within field).\\n\\nBLOCK C: PLACEBOS AND RIVAL CENTRALITIES. (C1) Rewired backbone. 200 draws of networkx.connected_double_edge_swap on the 78-edge positive-phi graph (nswap = 10 x edges, seeded), with weights CARRIED by their edges. This preserves the degree sequence and connectivity, so eigenvector centrality is always defined. A variant keeps the rewired topology and shuffles the weights across edges. For each draw, recompute the eigenvector gateway (normalised to max 1), re-attach it to the rows, and compute point delta-AUC (M0 + g and M2 + g). Report the placebo distribution (median, 95th percentile), the real value's percentile and an empirical p = (1 + #placebo >= real)/201. Also report the median Spearman correlation between the real and rewired gateway vectors (the placebo's discriminating power): if it is > 0.8, report that degree-preserving rewiring cannot separate eigenvector position from degree on a 26-node graph. (C2) Node-label permutation. Over 1,000 permutations of the 26 gateway values across fields, compute delta-AUC under M2. This is the 'any arbitrary field constant' null and the most important placebo for a field-level regressor. (C3) Rival centralities on the same backbone: weighted degree (strength), binary degree, betweenness (distance = 1/phi), PageRank (alpha 0.85), closeness, k-core number, eigenvector on phi_min, and log_field_size. Each is added singly to M2 on exp4, the union panel and the new-episodes panel, with refit-bootstrap 95% CIs (2,000 draws on the union panel, 500 elsewhere if time is short, noted) and Holm correction over the 8 rivals plus gateway. Also report the 9x9 Spearman matrix across the 26 fields.\\n\\nBLOCK D: THE O1 ARTEFACT. Using exp4's outcomes.csv and features.csv and the same concept-level LOGO logistic model as exp4's secondary_screens (46 concepts), recompute delta-AUC on O1 for G, G_all, G_deg, G_btw, G_phimin, G_A, REL_home and DOM_Social. Three baselines: B5 (reproduce the reported +0.07..+0.15); B5 + label_coverage_early; and B5 + label_coverage_early + O1_base. O1_base is the training-fold mean O1 of concepts sharing the home field, falling back to the training-fold global mean, so it is leakage-free. Refit concept bootstrap (2,000), 90% and 95% CIs, and groups positive. Also report Spearman(G variant, label_coverage_early) and the partial correlation with O1 given coverage. A variant's O1 gain is called an ARTEFACT if its delta falls by >= 50% and its 90% CI then includes 0.\\n\\nBLOCK E: POWER. Inputs: SE_boot, the refit-bootstrap SD of delta-AUC for gateway_j over M2 on the union panel, together with its n_rows, n_concepts and mean episodes per concept m0; and the within-concept ICC rho_c of R, taken as the latent-scale ICC of a concept random-intercept logit on M2 residual structure (or the ANOVA estimator on the Pearson residuals). Design effect DE(m) = 1 + (m - 1) rho_c. Analytic scaling: SE(N, m) = SE_boot x sqrt[(N0/DE(m0)) / (N/DE(m))]. MDE(N, m) = (1.96 + 0.84) x SE(N, m) for N in {1,000; 2,000; 4,000} episodes and m in {5, 10}. Simulation check (500 sims per cell, seeded): generate episodes from the fitted M2 + gateway model with a concept random intercept of variance matched to rho_c, using covariates bootstrapped from the union panel and gateway values from the 26-field backbone. Fit with 4-fold grouped CV and record the sampling SD of delta-AUC. Power at a true delta = 0.05 (the pre-registered H1 bar) and at the shrunken estimate (lower 90% bound on the union panel) is Phi(delta/SE_sim - 1.96). Held-out sizing: the number of concepts per held-out group needed so that P(group delta > 0) >= 0.9 per group and P(>= 3 of 4 groups positive) >= 0.8, assuming independent groups and per-group SE scaled from the per-group refit SEs.\\n\\nBLOCK F: CORRECTED RECORD TABLES. Each table gets one CI convention, stated in its header. F1: rho_B5 per experiment (0.834 exp1, 0.770 exp3, 0.327 exp4) with per-group values and n, read from each screen_result.json; exp4's own base rho is the value under 'delta_rho_O2r_m30.base'. F2: A*_h median and IQR per dev group from exp1 features.csv (A_h), with the count of negative medians. F3: exp3's full 34-row within-group portability table, verbatim from screen_result.json['portability'], with each row's CI convention noted. F4: exp4's full G secondary_screens table (O2r_m30, O1, O3 x 12 variants + joint model), flagging variants whose 90% CI lies wholly below 0 (G_all -0.240 [-0.419, -0.087]; DOM_Physical -0.110 [-0.193, -0.037]; GATEWAY_REACH -0.216 [-0.406, -0.048]) and noting that O3 is not evaluable (2 positives). F5: exp4 field-level rows with EXACT feature lists read from method.py lines 317-326 (all_four_available = B_field + {gateway_j, phi_home_j, density_j}, i.e. THREE added features and no size control, delta 0.082; size_controlled_all_three = B_field + log_field_size + the same three, delta 0.085). Each iteration-1 figure is shown next to its new refit-bootstrap CI.\",", "+  \"metrics_descriptions\": \"UNIT AND OUTCOME. One row is an adoption episode (concept c, off-home field j). Each is added singly to M2 on exp4, the union panel and the new-episodes panel, with refit-bootstrap 95% CIs (2,000 draws on the union panel, 500 elsewhere if time is short, noted) and Holm correction over the 8 rivals plus gateway. Also report the 9x9 Spearman matrix across the 26 fields.\\n\\nBLOCK D: THE O1 ARTEFACT. Using exp4's outcomes.csv and features.csv and the same concept-level LOGO logistic model as exp4's secondary_screens (46 concepts), recompute delta-AUC on O1 for G, G_all, G_deg, G_btw, G_phimin, G_A, REL_home and DOM_Social. Three baselines: B5 (reproduce the reported +0.07..+0.15); B5 + label_coverage_early; and B5 + label_coverage_early + O1_base. O1_base is the training-fold mean O1 of concepts sharing the home field, falling back to the training-fold global mean, so it is leakage-free. Refit concept bootstrap (2,000), 90% and 95% CIs, and groups positive. Also report Spearman(G variant, label_coverage_early) and the partial correlation with O1 given coverage. A variant's O1 gain is called an ARTEFACT if its delta falls by >= 50% and its 90% CI then includes 0.\\n\\nBLOCK E: POWER. Inputs: SE_boot, the refit-bootstrap SD of delta-AUC for gateway_j over M2 on the union panel, together with its n_rows, n_concepts and mean episodes per concept m0; and the within-concept ICC rho_c of R, taken as the latent-scale ICC of a concept random-intercept logit on M2 residual structure (or the ANOVA estimator on the Pearson residuals). Design effect DE(m) = 1 + (m - 1) rho_c. Analytic scaling: SE(N, m) = SE_boot x sqrt[(N0/DE(m0)) / (N/DE(m))]. MDE(N, m) = (1.96 + 0.84) x SE(N, m) for N in {1,000; 2,000; 4,000} episodes and m in {5, 10}. Simulation check (500 sims per cell, seeded): generate episodes from the fitted M2 + gateway model with a concept random intercept of variance matched to rho_c, using covariates bootstrapped from the union panel and gateway values from the 26-field backbone. Fit with 4-fold grouped CV and record the sampling SD of delta-AUC. Power at a true delta = 0.05 (the pre-registered H1 bar) and at the shrunken estimate (lower 90% bound on the union panel) is Phi(delta/SE_sim - 1.96). Held-out sizing: the number of concepts per held-out group needed so that P(group delta > 0) >= 0.9 per group and P(>= 3 of 4 groups positive) >= 0.8, assuming independent groups and per-group SE scaled from the per-group refit SEs.\\n\\nBLOCK F: CORRECTED RECORD TABLES. Each table gets one CI convention, stated in its header. F1: rho_B5 per experiment (0.834 exp1, 0.770 exp3, 0.327 exp4) with per-group values and n, read from each screen_result.json; exp4's own base rho is the value under 'delta_rho_O2r_m30.base'. F2: A*_h median and IQR per dev group from exp1 features.csv (A_h), with the count of negative medians. F3: exp3's full 34-row within-group portability table, verbatim from screen_result.json['portability'], with each row's CI convention noted. F4: exp4's full G secondary_screens table (O2r_m30, O1, O3 x 12 variants + joint model), flagging variants whose 90% CI lies wholly below 0 (G_all -0.240 [-0.419, -0.087]; DOM_Physical -0.110 [-0.193, -0.037]; GATEWAY_REACH -0.216 [-0.406, -0.048]) and noting that O3 is not evaluable (2 positives). F5: exp4 field-level rows with EXACT feature lists read from method.py lines 317-326 (all_four_available = B_field + {gateway_j, phi_home_j, density_j}, i.e. THREE added features and no size control, delta 0.082; size_controlled_all_three = B_field + log_field_size + the same three, delta 0.085). Each iteration-1 figure is shown next to its new refit-bootstrap CI.\\n\\nEXECUTION ORDER AND TIME (3 h total; CPU only; no API calls of any kind, so do NOT use the OpenAlex key or OpenRouter). 0:00-0:10: write verdict_ladder.json and crosswalk.json (pre-registration) BEFORE loading any R column into a model. 0:10-0:30: Step 0 plus the exact reproduction of exp4's 0.10254 and 0.10222 (if these do not reproduce to 1e-4, stop and debug the join before continuing). 0:30-1:10: Block A (parallelise the bootstrap draws with ProcessPoolExecutor, 4 workers, OMP_NUM_THREADS=1; about 16k small logistic fits per 2,000-draw analysis). 1:10-1:40: Block B (B3 loads scan/ckpt.npz; nt about 4,516 topics, so aggregate with np.bincount over field-pair indices and do not densify nt x nt beyond one int32 vector per slice). 1:40-2:05: Block C (C1 uses point estimates only, 200 x 2 fits; C2 uses 1,000 point estimates). 2:05-2:20: Block D. 2:20-2:35: Block E. 2:35-3:00: Block F, figures, eval_out.json, README.md and .aii/manifest.yaml. Scaling rule (aii-long-running-tasks): run every bootstrap first with 50 draws and time it; if the extrapolated total exceeds the slot, drop the secondary datasets to 500 draws but keep 2,000 for the union panel and the new-episodes panel, and record it. FIGURES (aii-data-fig-gen): a forest plot of delta-AUC by dataset x group with the refit 95% CIs and the RE pooled diamond; placebo histograms for C1 and C2 with the real value marked; a stage-2 scatter of field intercepts against gateway_j; and an MDE-versus-N curve for m = 5 and 10. OUTPUT: eval_out.json validated with aii-json against the exp_eval_sol_out schema (read the schema first; the per-episode rows with OOF predictions for M2 and M2 + gateway go in the datasets/examples part, and every table above goes in the aggregate metrics with keys A_replication, B_trait, C_placebo, D_O1_artefact, E_power, F_record, overlap, verdict, missing_inputs, deviations), plus mini/preview variants. Write the harmonised union panel to results/union_episodes.csv so iteration 2 can reuse it. FAILURE HANDLING: a field name that cannot be crosswalked -> drop the row, count it, and do not guess; eigenvector centrality that fails to converge on a rewired graph -> redraw with a new seed (connected swaps should prevent this); a group with one class in a fold -> report NA for that group and never impute an AUC; B3 gates failing -> report NOT IDENTIFIABLE with the gate values; exp3 scan files missing -> skip B3 only. Everything written stays inside the workspace; manifest: everything here is small text, except any cached aggregated npz, which goes under delete: regenerable with the command that rebuilds it.\",", "   \"metrics_justification\": \"The hypothesis now stands on one lead: +0.103 delta-AUC for gateway_j on 80 episodes and 28 concepts, in one experiment, with a fixed-prediction CI and no control for the adopting field's own retention habit. Every block targets one way that lead could be false. (1) Replication on units that were labelled differently (s2-fos text classifier; snapshot title-matched venue labels) tests whether the effect is an artefact of exp4's label pipeline or its top-200-source truncation. The new-episodes-only panel and the overlap report stop the three files from being counted as three independent confirmations when they share concepts. (2) The refit, concept-clustered bootstrap is the only CI that reflects both pseudo-replication (several fields per concept) and model-selection variance. The fixed-prediction CI understates both, and the reviewer flagged it. (3) gateway_j is a pure field constant, so it can only work by ranking fields. The decisive rivals are therefore other field constants: the field's leave-concept-out retention propensity (does the field keep everything?), its size, arbitrary permuted field scores (C2) and other centralities (C3). If gateway_j loses to P_cj or does no better than permuted field scores, the right reading is 'retention is a trait of the adopting field', which the hypothesis already names as an informative outcome. B2 tells that result apart from a genuine positional effect: gateway position explains the field intercepts. (4) The rewired-backbone placebo (C1) tests the network claim itself. The rescue-effect mechanism says position in the relatedness web matters, not just the number of neighbours. On a 26-node graph, degree-preserving rewiring may keep eigenvector ranks, so the plan reports how well the placebo can discriminate instead of over-reading a pass. (5) The O1 re-screen closes a flagged loose end: the G variants' O1 gains may come from label coverage, because G correlates 0.20 with coverage. (6) The power block turns the lead into the sample-size decision for the iteration-2 panel. The iteration-1 positive-control ladder showed that underpowered screens waste iterations. The MDE uses the shrunken effect and the pre-registered 0.05 bar, not the winner's-curse +0.10. (7) The corrected tables are what the paper cites. The pre-registered VERDICT LADDER, written into eval_out.json before Block A runs: REPLICATES = union and new-episode delta-AUC over M2 > 0 with 95% refit CI > 0, same sign in >= 3 of 4 groups, surviving P_cj, and above the 95th percentile of C2. ATTENUATES = positive point estimates, but a CI including 0 on the new-episodes panel or after P_cj. FIELD-TRAIT = P_cj alone carries the gain and gateway_j adds <= 0.01 given P_cj, or gateway_j sits inside the C2 null. FAILS = new-episodes delta <= 0. All four verdicts are reportable and feed the iteration-2 decision.\",", "   \"domain_practice\": \"What the field does for this kind of study (relatedness/diversification entry-exit prediction in scientometrics and economic complexity, evaluated as held-out field-level retention). This rests on the strategist's field reasoning, on my reading this session of the three iteration-1 artifacts' code and results (screen.py, backbone.py, method.py, screen_result.json and the field_outcomes files), and on standard references I know but did not re-open this session: Hidalgo et al. 2007 Science and 2018 'The principle of relatedness'; Guevara et al. 2016 Scientometrics 'research space'; Neffke, Henning & Boschma 2011; Maslov & Sneppen 2002; Field & Welsh 2007 on cluster bootstraps; Obuchowski 1997 on clustered AUC. BASELINES. Every relatedness paper compares density-to-current-portfolio with a size or ubiquity control. Entry and retention regressions carry LOCATION and ACTIVITY fixed effects (here field and concept), and AUC of entry against a random or size-only predictor is the standard predictive readout (Hidalgo 2007; Guevara 2016 report AUC around 0.7-0.8 for field entry). The baseline a reviewer names first here is RELATEDNESS DENSITY / relatedness-to-origin plus FIELD SIZE, followed by the 'unit has a trait' control: field FE or a leave-one-out field propensity. CONTROLS. The network-science norm for a centrality claim is a degree-preserving (configuration or Maslov-Sneppen) null, with connectivity preserved when eigenvector centrality is used. For a node-level constant attached to many observations, the norm is a node-label permutation test, because any field constant can soak up field heterogeneity. HOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Scientometric predictive claims resting on under 100 units or under about 30 clusters are treated as exploratory. Uncertainty is reported with cluster-robust or cluster-bootstrap intervals at the level of the independent unit (here the concept), and heterogeneity across domains is reported (I^2) rather than averaged away. Design effects 1 + (m-1)ICC are the standard way to size clustered samples. With 26 fields, any field-level coefficient has at most 26 effective between-field units, which caps what a static field regressor can show. MEASURES AND REPORTING. AUC and delta-AUC with bootstrap CIs, a standardised coefficient, per-domain tables, random-effects pooling, Holm correction across a family of rival indicators, and reporting of negative and one-domain-only results. Known data caveats: venue-label coverage varies by field (26-80%), s2-fos is a text classifier and not a venue label, and top-N source truncation biases late shares.\",", "   \"practice_alignment\": \"MEETS: (1) the relatedness baseline (phi_home_j, density_j) and field-size controls are in every model (M2); (2) the trait control is present twice, as a leave-concept-out training-fold propensity and as a field-intercept decomposition, and the time-varying FE design is attempted; (3) the degree- and connectivity-preserving rewired null and a node-label permutation null are both run; (4) concept-clustered refit bootstraps with 2,000 draws are the only new CIs; (5) per-group results, random-effects pooling with I^2, and Holm correction over the rival centralities are all reported; (6) negative variants (G_all, DOM_Physical, GATEWAY_REACH) are tabulated, not dropped. DEPARTS: (a) No held-out fields: all rows are dev groups (CS, Eng, BGM, Med) with onset 2003-2009, so this is an internal stress test, not confirmation. That is justified because the direction is zero-cost on existing outputs, and held-out confirmation is owned by the iteration-2 common-panel experiment and its held-out scoring. Cost: at best, a 'replicates on dev' verdict. (b) The replications are only semi-independent: the three files share most P78 concepts and the same R rule. The new-episodes-only panel and the overlap report quantify this, but it still inflates apparent consistency. (c) Static field FE is unidentifiable for a field constant. It is replaced by propensity plus the two-stage decomposition, and the time-varying FE may be declared non-identifiable because only two slices and small within-field centrality change are available. The cost is that the strongest version of the trait test is deferred to iteration 2, and the output says so explicitly. (d) The s2-fos to OpenAlex crosswalk is hand-specified and many-to-one for Biology and the social sciences. A crosswalk-clean sensitivity bounds this. (e) The 26-node backbone makes the rewired placebo weak (eigenvector ranks may survive rewiring); its discriminating power is reported instead of claiming a pass. (f) There is no insularity covariate (never computed in iteration 1) and no fresh label-coverage covariate for exp1/exp3 field rows beyond what their files hold; this is listed as a residual confound. (g) Power uses the dev-panel ICC and effect, which may be optimistic; the plan uses the lower 90% bound and the 0.05 bar to counter the winner's curse. Remaining credibility cost: a positive result here licenses spending on the held-out panel; it is not a finding.\""]}], "userModified": false, "replaceAll": false}

### [78] ASSISTANT · 2026-09-28 17:02:58 UTC

```
I've written the evaluation plan to `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`. It fills all 8 schema fields. It is a stress test of the one surviving lead: the adopting field's gateway centrality adds about +0.10 AUC to predicting whether that field keeps publishing on a concept. That number comes from 80 concept × field rows in one experiment. The plan needs no OpenAlex credits and no OpenRouter spend, and runs on the `cpu_plus` profile. I checked the JSON's structure by pattern search only; it hasn't been through a JSON parser.

Reading the three iteration-1 workspaces changed four things in the plan:

- **Field fixed effects can't test this effect as written.** Gateway centrality is one fixed number per field, so it is perfectly collinear with field dummies. The plan replaces that test with three others:
  - the field's retention rate on the other concepts, computed from training data only;
  - a check of whether gateway centrality explains the per-field intercepts, with a permutation test;
  - a time-varying version built from experiment 3's cached topic-pair counts for 2000–04 and 2005–09. Two gates are set in advance: its centrality must match experiment 4's backbone, and fields must change enough between slices. If either gate fails, the result is recorded as "not identifiable" and the test passes to the iteration-2 panel.
- **Experiment 1 uses different field names.** Its 367 rows use 23 Semantic Scholar categories, not OpenAlex fields, and the only existing mapping covers 4 of them. The plan fixes a full crosswalk before any model is fitted, plus a sensitivity run that drops the ambiguous rows (Biology, Geography, the social sciences).
- **The "replications" share most concepts.** All three files use the same 78-concept dev panel and the same retention rule. The plan reports the overlap and adds a panel of only the episodes that are new relative to experiment 4. That is the cleanest replication available.
- **The rewiring placebo may be weak.** The backbone has only 26 fields and 78 edges, so rewiring that keeps each field's connection count may barely change centrality ranks. The plan therefore reports how much each rewired centrality vector still correlates with the real one. It also adds a second null: 1,000 random shuffles of the centrality values across fields, which tests whether any arbitrary field-level number does as well.

The rest of the plan:

- **Replication:** each experiment's own baseline, then the concept-level baseline, then relatedness and size controls. Intervals come from a 2,000-draw bootstrap that resamples concepts and refits the models. The run must first reproduce experiment 4's reported 0.10254 and 0.10222 exactly before continuing.
- **O1 re-check:** the O1 gains of the gateway variants are re-scored with label coverage and a training-fold O1 base rate added to the baseline.
- **Power:** the smallest detectable AUC gain for 1,000, 2,000 and 4,000 episodes, and the held-out concepts needed per group. It is sized on the shrunken effect and the pre-registered 0.05 bar, not on +0.10.
- **Corrected tables:** baseline strength per experiment, A*_h medians, the 34-row portability table, the negative variants, and the field-level rows relabelled with their exact feature lists.
- **Verdicts:** four outcomes are written down before any model runs: replicates, attenuates, field-trait, fails.

Two gaps can't be closed with iteration-1 data, and the plan says so in its practice-alignment field. Every row is from the development groups, so the best possible outcome is "replicates on dev", not confirmation. And there is no field insularity covariate, because iteration 1 never computed one.

The field-practice section rests on the three iteration-1 workspaces, which I read, and on standard references I cited without reopening them. There was no web search.
```
